KeySpan Gas East Corporation, The Brooklyn Union Gas Company, National Grid USA Service Company 
Report to Management 
1 
Executive summary 
This document outlines the approach and results of PricewaterhouseCoopers’ engagement to examine certain 
costs incurred by operating subsidiaries of National Grid USA (the Company). This document is presented to 
provide management with an appropriate understanding of our procedures and our findings.  
Our engagement 
Our engagement is performed under the attestation standards of the American Institute of Certified Public 
Accountants. We have been engaged to perform an examination of financial information presented by the 
Company in accordance with sufficient and reasonable criteria prepared by the Company. Management will 
assert to the accuracy of the financial information; our opinion will attest to the accuracy of management’s 
assertions in all material respects. 
Scope of our examination 
Our examination is designed to cover subject matter that includes transactions recorded by the following 
subsidiaries of the Company: 
■ 
KeySpan Gas East Corporation (KEDLI)  
■ 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company (KEDNY) 
■ 
National Grid USA Service Company (Service Company) 
For each entity, the examination report comprises a Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the three-
month periods ended December 31, 2014, March 31, 2015, June 30, 2015, and September 30, 2015, and a 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs, Employee Expense Costs, and Operating Expense General Ledger Entries for 
the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015, with accompanying notes, together referred to as the 
Summary of Costs. 
Management’s responsibilities 
As part of the engagement process, management is responsible for the following: 
■ 
The Company’s financial information; 
■ 
The basis of presentation criteria upon which management’s assertions are based. 
Risk and approach 
Our procedures are designed to test management’s assertions and address the risk that management’s 
assertions are materially inaccurate. We have outlined our procedures in response to these risks on page 3. 
Results and findings 
Our results and findings are detailed on pages 4 to 28. The findings include amounts related to operating 
expense general ledger entries of $2.01 million related to KEDLI (page 12), $2.39 million related to KEDNY 
(page 19) and $3.01 million related to Service Company (page 28). The findings also include amounts related to 
employee expenses of $26 thousand related to KEDNY (page 18). Management has reflected each of these 
amounts as an “adjustment to reflect exceptions identified under this examination” in the Summary of Costs for 
KEDLI, KEDNY and Service Company, respectively. Management has concluded that the remaining exceptions 
on pages 4-11, 13-17 and 20-27 are immaterial for adjustment in the respective Summary of Costs reports. 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                   Page 4 of 69
174


--- Page 181 ---
KeySpan Gas East Corporation, The Brooklyn Union Gas Company, National Grid USA Service Company 
Report to Management 
 
2 
Risk and Approach 
The subject matter included in the Summary of Costs reports was developed as follows: 
Vendor costs - Management provided to PricewaterhouseCoopers LLP (PwC) a list of amounts 
approved for payment to each vendor in each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were selected 
that represent the top 25 vendors based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 15 vendors 
selected without bias that are ranked between 26 and 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each 
quarter, and 10 vendors selected without bias ranked greater than 100 based on dollars approved for 
payment in each quarter. The subject matter excludes amounts approved for payment related to 
purchased gas, as these costs generally are not recovered via base distribution rates. 
Labor costs (KEDLI and KEDNY) – Management provided to PwC a report including base time and 
overtime pay, but excluding labor-related overhead costs such as social security taxes, life insurance, 
pensions, post-retirement benefits other than pension, medical, dental and other welfare expenses. All 
base time and overtime pay was selected for inclusion in the subject matter. 
Labor costs (Service Company) – Management provided to PwC a report including base time and 
overtime pay, but excluding labor-related overhead costs such as social security taxes, life insurance, 
pensions, post-retirement benefits other than pension, medical, dental and other welfare expenses. Based 
upon Management’s list, items were selected that represented cost centers within the 20 largest operating 
departments as determined by payroll cost incurred, and 20 departments selected without bias ranked 
greater than 20, based on payroll cost incurred. 
Employee expense costs – Management provided to PwC a list of amounts approved for payment in 
each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were selected that represent the top 25 single 
employee claims (“trip reports”) and top 50 purchase credit card (“P-card”) transactions based on dollars 
approved for payment in each quarter, 15 trip reports selected without bias ranked between 26 and 100 
based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 10 trip reports selected without bias ranked 
greater than 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, and 25 P-card transactions 
selected without bias ranked greater than 25 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter. The 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs does not include any expenses submitted for 
reimbursement by “Band A” employees, defined as senior executives and senior vice presidents of 
NGUSA. 
Operating expense general ledger entries – Management provided to PwC a list of all journal 
entries that impacted operating expense general ledger accounts. For each system “document type” 
included in the list, items were identified for selection and presentation in the Summary of Selected 
Operating Expense General Ledger Entries based on dollar value, as detailed in Note 6 to each of the 
Summary of Costs reports.

KeySpan Gas East Corporation, The Brooklyn Union Gas Company, National Grid USA Service Company Report to Management 
 
Risk and Approach (continued) 
 
 
3 
Cost area 
Test objective 
Test number and details 
Vendor  
To test management’s assertion that 
vendor costs are incurred and 
accurate 
1. Compare cost recorded in ledger to underlying vendor 
support (invoice or similar.) 
 
To test management’s assertion that 
vendor costs are allocated to the 
correct company 
2. Review the underlying vendor information for details of the 
services performed and identify whether services relate to 
the entity to which they are charged. 
 
To test management’s assertion that 
costs charged to “C” projects are in 
support of the capital program, and 
that costs charged to other projects 
are expense related (KEDLI and 
KEDNY only) 
3. Review the underlying vendor information for details of the 
services performed and identify whether services are 
performed in support of the capital program or not. 
Payroll 
To test management’s assertion that 
payroll costs are accurate 
4. Select a sample of employee payments during the period and 
test that the amount paid is correct by recalculating the 
amount with reference to employee contractual 
arrangements. 
5. Review monthly payroll totals and identify any outlier 
information; obtain explanations for any unusual 
fluctuations and test that they are reasonable. 
 
To test management’s assertion that 
payroll costs are charged to Service 
Company orders consistent with the 
work performed (Service Company 
only) 
6. Review amounts charged by employees charging Service 
Company “X orders” and test whether the allocation charged 
is consistent with the employee’s job title and functional 
area.  
 
To test management’s assertion that 
payroll costs are properly allocated 
among capital and expense, and that 
costs are allocated to the correct 
companies (KEDLI and KEDNY 
only) 
7. Obtain and reperform management’s “KC2” analytical 
procedure; obtain explanations for any unusual fluctuations 
and test that they are reasonable. 
8. Select a sample of employees and review their time capture 
records; apply metrics to identify outlier entries (e.g., more 
than 12 hours in a day, hours worked between midnight and 
4am, multiple entities charged in one day); obtain 
explanations for any unusual fluctuations and test that they 
are reasonable. 
Employee 
expenses 
To test management’s assertion that 
employee expenses are accurate and 
submitted consistent with the 
Company policy 
9. Obtain the claim information and supporting documents 
submitted; compare such documents to the requirements of 
the Company policy 
Journal 
entries 
To test management’s assertion that 
the journal entries presented are 
accurately calculated and reflect the 
underlying transaction 
10. Obtain copies of the approved journal entry submission and 
test that the entry ties to the underlying support. Review and 
recalculate elements of the underlying support to test its 
accuracy.

9 
Results and Findings – Vendor Costs – KeySpan Gas East 
Corporation (continued) 
We considered whether there were any capital program or non-capital program exceptions. A capital 
program exception represents a cost which was not part of a capital program but was charged to a work 
order implemented under a “C” capital program project; a non-capital program exception represents a 
cost which was part of a capital program but was charged to a work order coded under an expense-only 
project. 
There were no capital program or non-capital program exceptions identified in KEDLI.

Report of Independent Accountants 
 
 
To the Management  
of National Grid USA  
 
We have examined the assertion made by management of National Grid USA (NGUSA) that the accompanying 
 
 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the three-month periods ended December 31, 2014, March 
31, 2015, June 30, 2015, and September 30, 2015 
 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 
 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 
 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries for the twelve-month period ended 
September 30, 2015 
 
(collectively, the “Summary of Costs”) of National Grid USA Service Company, included on pages 10 to 16, are 
each an accurate presentation of costs for the periods stated based on the criteria set forth in the notes to the 
Summary of Costs included on pages 3 to 9. Management of NGUSA is responsible for the assertion. Our 
responsibility is to express an opinion based on our examination.  
 
Our examination was conducted in accordance with attestation standards established by the American Institute 
of Certified Public Accountants and, accordingly, included examining, on a test basis, evidence supporting 
management’s assertion and performing such other procedures as we considered necessary in the 
circumstances. We believe that our examination provides a reasonable basis for our opinion.  
 
Management's assertion and our examination were limited to the accuracy of the information presented in the 
Summary of Costs. Management's assertion and our examination did not consider the completeness of the 
information contained in the Summary of Costs. Additionally, our examination did not include the 
supplemental information to the Summary of Costs included on pages 17 to 18, accordingly, we do not express 
an opinion or provide any assurance on it. 
 
In our opinion, the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs, Summary of Selected Labor Costs, Summary of Selected 
Employee Expense Costs, and Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries (collectively, as 
defined above, the Summary of Costs) of National Grid USA Service Company are, in all material respects, 
accurate costs for each of the periods stated above, based on the criteria set forth in the notes to the Summary 
of Costs.  
 
 
 
 
New York, New York

Summary of Cos 
Notes to summary of costs 
1. Management’s Assertion 
 
Management of National Grid USA (NGUSA) asserts that the accompanying:  
 
• 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the three-month periods ended December 31, 2014, 
March 31, 2015, June 30, 2015, and September 30, 2015 (see pages 10-13) 
• 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 (see page 14) 
• 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 
(see page 15) 
• 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries for the twelve-month period ended 
September 30, 2015 (see page 16) 
 
(collectively, the “Summary of Costs”) of National Grid USA Service Company (Servco, or the Company) are an 
accurate presentation of costs for the periods stated based on the criteria set forth in the notes below. 
 
2. Background 
NGUSA is a public utility holding company with regulated subsidiaries engaged in the generation of electricity 
and the transmission, distribution, and sale of both natural gas and electricity. NGUSA is a direct wholly-
owned subsidiary of National Grid North America, Inc. (NGNA) and an indirect wholly-owned subsidiary of 
National Grid plc, a public limited company incorporated under the laws of England and Wales. 
Servco, a wholly-owned subsidiary of NGUSA, provides management, administrative, accounting, legal, 
engineering, and other services to other NGUSA subsidiaries. Servco provides its services on an “at cost” basis, 
determined using billing methods based on cost causative factors pursuant to service agreements and its Cost 
Allocation Manual, as further discussed in Note 7. 
NGUSA and its subsidiaries separately issue financial statements prepared in accordance with accounting 
principles generally accepted in the United States (GAAP), as well as, for certain subsidiaries, financial 
statements prepared in accordance with the accounting requirements of the Federal Energy Regulatory 
Commission (FERC) as set forth in its applicable Uniform System of Accounts and published accounting 
releases (together, the FERC USoA). The subject matter presented in the Summary of Costs is prepared 
consistent with the “Basis of Accounting” described in notes 3 to 6 below, which are not necessarily the same 
as the accounting requirements of the FERC USoA or GAAP. 
3. Vendor Costs 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs – During each of the quarters in the period from October 1, 2014, to 
September 30, 2015, the Company approved payments to third party vendors totalling $1.5 billion 
(unaudited). The Summary of Selected Vendor Costs represents vendor costs “approved for payment” during 
each of the quarters in the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, and includes only amounts 
related to the selected vendors listed.  
This subject matter, designed to reflect a component of total vendor costs, was developed as follows. 
Management provided to PricewaterhouseCoopers LLP (PwC) a list of amounts approved for payment by 
Servco to each vendor in each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were selected that represent the 
top 25 vendors based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 15 vendors selected without bias that 
are ranked between 26 and 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, and 10 vendors 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                  Page 35 of 69
205


--- Page 212 ---
selected without bias ranked greater than 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter. The 
subject matter excludes amounts approved for payment related to purchased commodity and storm 
restoration, as these costs generally are not recovered via the base distribution rates established by affiliates 
of the Company, and those related to fleet vehicle lease payments.  
Basis of Accounting – Vendor costs included in the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the quarters 
in the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015 represent costs “approved for payment” for each 
named vendor during the period presented. Irrespective of when the goods or services were received, for 
purposes of this presentation, vendor costs are considered approved for payment upon: 
 
establishment of an approved purchase order,  
 
internal acknowledgment that the goods or services have been received, and 
 
receipt of a vendor invoice. 
All costs included in the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs have been “approved for payment” by the 
Company, and properly allocated and charged to the Company and its affiliates, in accordance with the 
Company’s Cost Allocation Manual. The Cost Allocation Manual is further described in Note 7. Note that 
vendor costs may have been incurred before the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, but if all 
three elements above were not present until after October 1, 2014 and prior to September 30, 2015, such 
costs would consequently be “approved for payment” during the period presented and included in the 
accompanying Summary of Selected Vendor Costs.  Likewise, vendor costs incurred during the period from 
October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, where all three elements above were not present prior to September 
30, 2015, are not considered “approved for payment” during the period presented, nor included in the 
accompanying Summary of Selected Vendor Costs. This accounting policy, applied in the Summary of Costs, is 
a basis of accounting other than GAAP and the FERC USoA.  
4. Labor Costs 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs – During the period from October 1, 2014, to September 30, 2015, the 
Company recorded costs to employees of $481 million (unaudited) in consideration for work undertaken for 
the benefit of the Company and its affiliates. This amount includes base time and overtime pay but does not 
include labor-related overhead costs such as social security taxes, life insurance, pensions, post-retirement 
benefits other than pension, medical, dental and other welfare expenses.  
The subject matter presented in the Summary of Selected Labor Costs was developed as follows. Management 
provided to PwC a list of payroll charged by Servco employees for the period from October 1, 2014, to 
September 30, 2015. Based upon Management's list, items were selected representing base time and 
overtime charged to Servco operating expense work orders, recorded by cost centers identified within the 20 
largest operating departments as determined by payroll cost incurred, and 20 departments selected without 
bias ranked greater than 20, based on payroll cost incurred. 
Employees of the Company and its affiliates charge time to system work orders, similar to the process 
described above for vendor costs. Labor costs are recorded by the Company’s payroll and time capture 
system. 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Labor Costs represent amounts 
recorded with respect to base time and overtime by employees of the Company for time charged during the 
period presented in the performance of activities for the benefit of the Company and its affiliates within the 
cost centers specified.  
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                  Page 36 of 69
206


--- Page 213 ---
Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Labor Costs have been properly allocated and charged to the 
Company and its affiliates, in accordance with the Company’s Cost Allocation Manual. The Cost Allocation 
Manual is further described in Note 7. 
5. Employee Expense Costs 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs – During the period from October 1, 2014, to September 30, 
2015, the Company approved payments to employees as reimbursement for business expenses incurred 
totalling $18 million (unaudited). Business expenses are approved for payment if they are incurred in the 
normal course of business, in accordance with the Company’s internal policies, and include business travel, 
accommodation, spot purchases of goods, and other out-of-pocket expenses. The Summary of Selected 
Employee Expense Costs includes a subset of such expenses. This subject matter, designed to reflect a 
component of total employee expense costs, was developed as follows. Management provided to PwC a list of 
amounts approved for payment by Servco in each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were 
selected that represent the top 25 single employee claims (“trip reports”) and top 50 purchase credit card (“P-
card”) transactions based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 15 trip reports selected without 
bias ranked between 26 and 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 10 trip reports 
selected without bias ranked greater than 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, and 25 
P-card transactions selected without bias ranked greater than 25 based on dollars approved for payment in 
each quarter. The Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs also only includes expenses submitted by 
certain employees; it does not include any expenses submitted for reimbursement by “Band A” employees, 
defined as senior executives and senior vice presidents of NGUSA. 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs represent 
employee expenses “approved for payment” during the period presented. Irrespective of when the expense 
was incurred, for purposes of this presentation, employee expenses are considered approved for payment 
upon submission by an employee of a request for reimbursement for a business expense, accompanied by 
evidence of the expense, and recoverable consistent with NGUSA’s US Business Travel & Expense Policy, 
effective January 18, 2011, updated March 1, 2013, with the exception of Section 19.1, which defines 
recoverable expenses that are subject to the P-card and other procurement guidelines (items recorded 
inconsistent with Section 19.1 were still included in the Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs if the 
business purpose for the expense was considered reasonable).  This accounting policy, applied in the Summary 
of Costs, is a basis of accounting other than GAAP and the FERC USoA. 
All costs included in the accompanying Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs have been allocated and 
charged to the Company and its affiliates, in a manner consistent with the Company’s Cost Allocation Manual. 
The Cost Allocation Manual is further described in Note 7. 
6. General Ledger Entries 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries – During the period from October 1, 2014 to 
September 30, 2015, the Company recorded entries to the general ledger via automated journal entry and 
manual journal entry processes. Entries in the Company’s general ledger are assigned a “document type” (doc 
type), a unique two-digit code, designed to identify the source and nature of the journal entry. The complete 
population of operating expenses recorded is an aggregation of amounts recorded under all doc types to the 
relevant general ledger accounts. Costs approved for payment to vendors are recorded in the general ledger 
under doc types KR, RE, RS, and KG; labor costs are recorded under doc type AB; and employee expense costs 
under doc types TR and YU. Entries recorded under the doc types KP, YV and WE are for the usage of 
materials, the inverse (receipt) of which is recorded initially as a RS or RE entry. 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                  Page 37 of 69
207


--- Page 214 ---
Additional entries to the general ledger to allow the Company to maintain underlying books and records on a 
GAAP and FERC USoA basis are recorded via manual or automated entry under separate doc types. 
Management provided to PwC a list of all amounts recorded as operating expense under all doc types, and the 
following items were identified for selection and presentation in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense 
General Ledger Entries: 
 
Amounts presented as “AB” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are a subset of entries recorded for manual adjustments to costs or internal orders for items other than 
labor costs. The Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries includes the impact of 24 
AB entries identified as the largest entries impacting the 5 largest general ledger line items (measured by 
absolute value).  
 
 
Amounts presented as “AC” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are manual accruals either reversing or accruing for costs incurred. The Summary of Selected Operating 
Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 9 AC accruing entries and largest 22 AC reversing 
entries (measured by absolute dollar impact on expense), and excluding amounts accruing and reversing 
in the twelve-month period.  
 


are manual journal entries to record manual adjustments to costs or internal orders. The Summary of 
Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 4 IG entries recorded measured 
by absolute dollar impact on expense.  
 
 
Amounts presented as “PS” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are entries recorded subsequent to the normal closing process, or as a result of audit adjustments. The 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 6 PS entries recorded 
measured by absolute dollar impact on expense. 
 
 
Amounts presented as “ZN” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are manual entries to record amortization of software expenses. The Summary of Selected Operating 
Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 9 ZN entries recorded during the period. 
 
 
Amounts presented as “02” in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries are 
credit entries to the income statement of the Company to reflect the allocation to the relevant 
counterparty of affiliate costs paid for by the Company. The other side of such entries is to an 
intercompany receivable. 
 
System-generated settlement entries recorded under doc type ST or SA are not considered for inclusion in the 
Summary of Costs as they are not typically subject to manual intervention. Entries recorded under doc type Z4 
(allowance for funds used during construction), doc type RV (miscellaneous customer billings), and doc type ZC 
(depreciation) are not considered for inclusion in the Summary of Costs as they are related to costs which, 
when presented for recovery in customer rates, are separately calculated without reference to historical data, 
or are recovered via mechanisms outside of base customer rates. Entries recorded under the doc type 01 are 
system-generated and amount to less than $8 million for the twelve-month period presented and have not 
been included in the Summary of Costs. Entries recorded under doc types WA and WL represent materials 
inventory recorded as costs when used; such costs are recorded at the same amount as the item was 
purchased (the purchase is initially recorded under a KR or RE entry - see description above and Note 3). 
Entries recorded under the doc types PR, RB, SK, WI, Y5, YT, Z5, ZJ, and ZQ aggregate under each individual doc 
type to an amount less than $1.5 million for the twelve-month period presented and have not been included 
in the Summary of Costs.

In addition, the entries included in the subject matter do not consider amounts recorded for purchased 
commodity and vehicle fleet leasing.  
 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger 
Entries are amounts recorded in the Company’s general ledger using the doc types listed during the period 
presented. The amounts presented represent the following: 
 


transaction. 


in the appropriate period of the underlying transaction. 


software costs.

7. Costs incurred and allocated by Servco 
Cost Allocation Manual and Service Agreements  
Costs incurred by Servco and by other NGUSA subsidiaries in providing services to NGUSA client companies 
(Client Companies) are set forth in the service agreements and its Cost Allocation Manual (Revised December 
2014) (CAM) and include, but are not limited to, labor and related overhead costs (e.g., salaries of officers and 
other employees, employee welfare expenses such as social security taxes, life insurance, pensions, post-
retirement benefits other than pension, medical, dental and other welfare expenses), training costs, facilities 
costs, information technology costs, and costs related to vendor and other contract services. The organization, 
conduct of business and method of cost allocation are designed to result in the performance of services and 
the provision of goods economically and efficiently for the benefit of the Client Companies at cost, fairly and 
equitably allocated among such companies. 
The purpose of the CAM is to serve as a guide as to the manner in which costs should be charged to the Client 
Companies by the company incurring the cost.  The prevailing premise of these cost allocation guidelines is 
that allocation methods should not result in subsidization of non-regulated services or products by regulated 
entities or subsidization of services or products from one regulated entity to another.   
The provision of administrative services to the Client Companies by Servco is specified in service agreements 
filed with the respective utility regulatory commissions. 
Principles of cost allocation 
The following principles guide the allocation of costs of products or services provided by Servco and other 
operating subsidiaries to the Client Companies.  
 
Direct charging or direct assignment is the preferred allocation methodology and is used if the cost of 
providing a product or service can be identified with the Client Companies receiving the benefit of that 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                  Page 39 of 69
209


--- Page 216 ---
product or service. Direct charging should only be used if the cost of providing a product or service to an 
individual Client Company can be isolated and reported separately from costs to provide other products 
or services and from costs to provide the same product and service to other Client Companies. 
 
The costs of products and services that cannot be direct charged should be allocated based on cost 
causative allocation bases representative of the underlying cost drivers of that product or service. 
 
The cost allocation methodology should be comprehensive, transparent, stable and administratively 
manageable and cost effective.   
 
The calculation of the cost allocation bases should be supported by a clearly defined methodology, model 
and supporting policy and procedure documentation.  
 
The cost allocation methodology should accommodate changes in the size of the allocation bases from 
period to period based on changes in the underlying cost drivers; the allocation bases should not vary 
significantly from period to period for uncontrollable factors not related to the underlying cost drivers.   
 
The calculation of the allocation bases should be updated at least annually and more frequently if needed 
to reflect significant events (e.g., the sale of a significant affiliate). 
Costs are defined among the following four categories for purposes of allocating the costs of products and 
services to the Client Companies.  This hierarchy represents the order of methodologies used when allocating 
costs.  These four cost categories are: 
 
Directly Assignable – Expenses incurred for activities and services exclusively for the benefit of a Client 
Company.   
 
Directly Attributable – Expenses incurred for activities and services that benefit more than one Client 
Company and which can be allocated based on direct measures of cost causation; for example number of 
employees or number of customers. 
 
Indirectly Attributable – Expenses incurred as a “cost of doing business” that do not relate to the 
provision of specific products and services.  The costs typically benefit all entities within the corporate 
umbrella.  Examples include governance costs, Corporate Secretary costs, and investor relations costs. 
These costs are typically allocated based on a general measure of cost causation, commonly referred to as 
the general allocator. Costs that cannot be directly identified with the provision of a particular product or 
service. This includes but is not limited to governance costs, insurance, and taxes as well the cost of 
services supporting Servco such as Servco accounting and recruiting for Servco positions.  The general 
allocator is the ratio of net plant, net margins and net operations and maintenance expenses, equally 
weighted. 
 
Unattributable – Expenses incurred for activities or services that have been determined as not 
appropriate for apportionment to the Client Companies.  These costs relate primarily to activities such as 
corporate diversification, political or philanthropic endeavors and, as such, are charged directly to the 
parent company, NGUSA. 
To apply these principles, Servco and the Client Companies accumulate costs in system orders. The order 
design acts as the primary cost control element from which costs are billed to affiliates. For each order, an 
order design documents a description of the order’s use and purpose, the activities associated with that 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                  Page 40 of 69
210


--- Page 217 ---
particular order, and justification for the allocation methodology to be used for allocating the costs 
accumulated in the order. Only one allocation methodology can be used for each order.  
Subsidiaries charge no more than actual costs for services provided. The monthly settlements process includes 
only the costs accumulated in the open orders. There is no markup or profit included in allocations to the 
regulated affiliates. The allocations are based on the methodologies designated and described above. 
Accordingly, the unit cost (price) charged to these affiliates is intended to reasonably approximate the actual 
costs of providing such services.  
Section 7 of the CAM lists those services provided by Servco and the Client Companies to whom these services 
are provided. These services are provided in accordance with the service agreements filed with the respective 
utility regulatory commissions.

Report of Independent Accountants 
 
 
To the Management  
of National Grid USA  
 
We have examined the assertion made by management of National Grid USA (NGUSA) that the 
accompanying 
 
 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the three-month periods ended  December 31, 
2014, March 31, 2015, June 30, 2015, and September 30, 2015 
 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 
 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 
2015 
 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries for the twelve-month period 
ended September 30, 2015 
 
(collectively, the “Summary of Costs”) of The Brooklyn Union Gas Company, included on pages 10 to 16, 
are each an accurate presentation of costs for the periods stated based on the criteria set forth in the notes 
to the Summary of Costs included on pages 3 to 9. Management of NGUSA is responsible for the 
assertion. Our responsibility is to express an opinion based on our examination.  
 
Our examination was conducted in accordance with attestation standards established by the American 
Institute of Certified Public Accountants and, accordingly, included examining, on a test basis, evidence 
supporting management’s assertion and performing such other procedures as we considered necessary in 
the circumstances. We believe that our examination provides a reasonable basis for our opinion.  
 
Management's assertion and our examination were limited to the accuracy of the information presented 
in the Summary of Costs. Management's assertion and our examination did not consider the 
completeness of the information contained in the Summary of Costs. Additionally, our examination did 
not include the supplemental information to the Summary of Costs included on pages 17 to 18, 
accordingly, we do not express an opinion or provide any assurance on it. 
 
In our opinion, the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs, Summary of Selected Labor Costs, Summary of 
Selected Employee Expense Costs, and Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
(collectively, as defined above, the Summary of Costs) of The Brooklyn Union Gas Company are, in all 
material respects, accurate costs for each of the periods stated above, based on the criteria set forth in the 
notes to the Summary of Costs.  
 
 
 
 
 
New York, New York 
December 18, 2015

Summary of Costs 
Notes to summary of costs 
1. Management’s Assertion 
 
Management of National Grid USA (NGUSA) asserts that the accompanying 
 
• 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the three-month periods ended December 31, 2014, 
March 31, 2015, June 30, 2015, and September 30, 2015 (see pages 10-13) 
• 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 2015 (see page 
14) 
• 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs for the twelve-month period ended September 30, 
2015 (see page 15) 
• 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries for the twelve-month period ended 
September 30, 2015 (see page 16) 
 
(collectively, the “Summary of Costs”) of The Brooklyn Union Gas Company (the Company) are each an 
accurate presentation of costs for the periods stated based on the criteria set forth in the notes below.   
 
2. Background 
The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a National Grid New York distributes natural gas to retail customers in 
the boroughs of Brooklyn and Staten Island and two-thirds of the borough of Queens, all in New York City.  
The Company is a wholly-owned subsidiary of KeySpan Corporation, which is a wholly-owned subsidiary of 
NGUSA. NGUSA is a public utility holding company with regulated subsidiaries engaged in the generation of 
electricity and the transmission, distribution, and sale of both natural gas and electricity. NGUSA is a direct 
wholly-owned subsidiary of National Grid North America Inc. (NGNA) and an indirect wholly-owned subsidiary 
of National Grid plc, a public limited company incorporated under the laws of England and Wales. 
National Grid USA Service Company (Servco), a wholly-owned subsidiary of NGUSA, provides management, 
administrative, accounting, legal, engineering, and other services to other NGUSA subsidiaries, including the 
Company. Servco provides its services on an “at cost” basis, determined using billing methods based on cost 
causative factors pursuant to service agreements and its Cost Allocation Manual, as further discussed in Note 
7. 
The Company separately issues financial statements prepared in accordance with the accounting 
requirements of the Federal Energy Regulatory Commission (FERC) as set forth in its applicable Uniform 
System of Accounts and published accounting releases (together, the FERC USoA) , as well as financial 
statements prepared in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States (GAAP). 
The subject matter presented in the Summary of Costs is prepared consistent with the “Basis of Accounting” 
described in notes 3 to 6 below, which are not necessarily the same as the accounting requirements of the 
FERC USoA or GAAP. 
3. Vendor Costs 
Summary of Selected Vendor Costs – During each of the quarters in the period from October 1, 2014 to 
September 30, 2015, the Company approved payments to third party vendors totalling $446 million 
(unaudited). The Summary of Selected Vendor Costs represents vendor costs “approved for payment” during 
each of the quarters in the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, related to the selected 
vendors listed.  
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 54 of 69
224


--- Page 231 ---
This subject matter, designed to reflect a component of total vendor costs, was developed as follows. 
Management provided to PricewaterhouseCoopers LLP (PwC) a list of amounts approved for payment by the 
Company to each vendor in each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were selected that represent 
the top 25 vendors based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 15 vendors selected without bias 
that are ranked between 26 and 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, and 10 vendors 
selected without bias ranked greater than 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter. The 
subject matter excludes amounts approved for payment related to purchased commodity and storm 
restoration, as these costs generally are not recovered via the Company’s base distribution rates. Also, for the 
following vendors, the subject matter includes only a subset of payments approved to the vendor: Hallen 
Construction (payments approved under order numbers with the largest 15 total amounts approved for 
payment each three-month period, equating to 1,028 invoices), New York Paving (payments approved under 
order numbers with the largest 15 total amounts approved for payment each three-month period, equating to 
459 invoices), McJunkin Red Man Corporation (largest 50 amounts approved for payment for each three-
month period), Dresser Piping Specialties (largest 50 amounts approved for payment for each three-month 
period), and Elster Perfection (largest 50 amounts approved for payment for the three-month period ended 
December 31, 2014 only). 
To capture and account for vendor costs, the Company implements work orders under two types of projects, 
(1) capital projects, under which work orders are designed to capture costs incurred in the execution of the 
Company’s capital programs, and (2) expense projects, under which work orders are designed to capture costs 
incurred in the performance of non-capital-related activity. The subject matter and basis of accounting reflects 
the initial determination of whether the vendor costs were assigned to capital projects or expense projects. 
System-generated entries (generally related to the allocation of engineering-related work order design 
adjustments, or the apportionment of overhead) and adjustments to allocate elements of work orders 
assigned to capital projects to expense, are not contemplated in the subject matter. 
Basis of Accounting – Vendor costs included in the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs for each of the quarters 
in the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015 represent costs “approved for payment” for each 
named vendor during the period presented. Irrespective of when the goods or services were received, for 
purposes of this presentation, vendor costs are considered approved for payment upon: 
 
 
establishment of an approved purchase order,  
 
internal acknowledgment that the goods or services have been received, and 
 
receipt of a vendor invoice. 
All costs included in the Summary of Selected Vendor Costs have been “approved for payment” by the 
Company, and properly allocated and charged to the Company and its affiliates, in accordance with the 
Company’s Cost Allocation Manual. The Cost Allocation Manual is further described in Note 7. Note that 
vendor costs may have been incurred before the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, but if all 
three elements above were not present until after October 1, 2014 and prior to September 30, 2015, such 
costs would consequently be “approved for payment” during the period presented and included in the 
accompanying Summary of Selected Vendor Costs.  Likewise, vendor costs incurred during the period from 
October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, where all three elements above were not present prior to September 
30, 2015, are not considered “approved for payment” during the period presented, nor included in the 
accompanying Summary of Selected Vendor Costs. This accounting policy, applied in the Summary of Costs, is 
a basis of accounting other than GAAP and the FERC USoA.  
 
Upon satisfaction of the conditions approving a cost for payment, the cost is assigned to a work order as 
follows:  
 
 
Costs incurred in support of the Company’s capital program are assigned to work orders capturing costs 
under capital projects.  
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 55 of 69
225


--- Page 232 ---
 
Costs related solely to operating activities are assigned to work orders capturing costs under expense 
projects, or internal orders capturing expense items specific to the Company and incurred outside of the 
Company’s capital program. 
 
4. Labor Costs 
Summary of Selected Labor Costs – During the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 2015, the 
Company recorded costs to employees of $119 million (unaudited) in consideration for work undertaken for 
the benefit of the Company. This amount includes base time and overtime pay but does not include labor-
related overhead costs such as social security taxes, life insurance, pensions, post-retirement benefits other 
than pension, medical, dental and other welfare expenses.  
Employees of the Company and its affiliates charge time to system work orders, similar to the process 
described above for vendor costs. Labor costs are recorded by the Company’s payroll and time capture 
system. Labor charges to work orders may be made by a direct employee of the Company, or by an employee 
of an affiliate company who is performing work for the benefit of the Company. 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Labor Costs represent amounts 
recorded with respect to base time and overtime by employees of the Company or its affiliates for time 
charged during the period presented and have been allocated consistent with the Company’s Cost Allocation 
Manual. Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Labor Costs have been charged to capital and 
operating cost line items within the general ledger in a manner consistent with nature of the work performed. 
 
5. Employee Expense Costs 
Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs – During the period from October 1, 2014 to September 30, 
2015, the Company approved payments to employees as reimbursement for business expenses incurred 
totalling $1 million (unaudited). Business expenses are approved for payment if they are incurred in the 
normal course of business, in accordance with the Company’s internal policies, and include business travel, 
accommodation, spot purchases of goods, and other out-of-pocket expenses. The Summary of Selected 
Employee Expense Costs includes a subset of such expenses. This subject matter, designed to reflect a 
component of total employee expense costs, was developed as follows. Management provided to PwC a list of 
amounts approved for payment by the Company in each quarter. Based upon Management's list, items were 
selected that represent the top 25 single employee claims (“trip reports”) and top 50 purchase credit card (“P-
card”) transactions based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 15 trip reports selected without 
bias ranked between 26 and 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, 10 trip reports 
selected without bias ranked greater than 100 based on dollars approved for payment in each quarter, and 25 
P-card transactions selected without bias ranked greater than 25 based on dollars approved for payment in 
each quarter. The Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs also only includes expenses submitted by 
certain employees; it does not include any expenses submitted for reimbursement by “Band A” employees, 
defined as senior executives and senior vice presidents of NGUSA. 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs represent 
employee expenses “approved for payment” during the period presented. Irrespective of when the expense 
was incurred, for purposes of this presentation, employee expenses are considered approved for payment 
upon submission by an employee of a request for reimbursement for a business expense, accompanied by 
evidence of the expense, and recoverable consistent with NGUSA’s US Business Travel & Expense Policy, 
effective January 18, 2011, updated March 1, 2013, with the exception of Section 19.1, which defines 
recoverable expenses that are subject to the P-card and other procurement guidelines (items recorded 
inconsistent with Section 19.1 were still included in the Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs if the 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 56 of 69
226


--- Page 233 ---
business purpose for the expense was considered reasonable).  This accounting policy, applied in the Summary 
of Costs, is a basis of accounting other than GAAP and the FERC USoA. 
 
All costs included in the accompanying Summary of Selected Employee Expense Costs have been allocated to 
the Company in a manner consistent with the Company’s Cost Allocation Manual. 
 
6. General Ledger Entries 
Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries – During the period from October 1, 2014 to 
September 30, 2015, the Company recorded entries to the general ledger via automated journal entry and 
manual journal entry processes. Entries in the Company’s general ledger are assigned a “document type” (doc 
type), a unique two-digit code, designed to identify the source and nature of the journal entry. The complete 
population of operating expenses recorded is an aggregation of amounts recorded under all doc types to the 
relevant general ledger accounts. Costs approved for payment to vendors are recorded in the general ledger 
under doc types KR, RE, RS, and KG; labor costs are recorded under doc type AB; and employee expense costs 
under doc types TR and YU. Entries recorded under the doc types KP, YV and WE are for the usage of 
materials, the inverse (receipt) of which is recorded initially as a RS or RE entry. 
Additional entries to the general ledger to allow the Company to maintain underlying books and records on a 
GAAP and FERC USoA basis are recorded via manual or automated entry under separate doc types. 
Management provided to PwC a list of all amounts recorded as operating expense under all doc types, and the 
following items were identified for selection and presentation in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense 
General Ledger Entries: 
 
Amounts presented as “AB” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are a subset of entries recorded for manual adjustments to costs or internal orders for items other than 
labor costs. The Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries includes the impact of 81 
AB entries identified as the largest entries impacting the 13 largest general ledger line items (measured by 
absolute value). Amounts related to property tax expense and regulatory amortizations were not included 
in the Summary. 
 
 
Amounts presented as “AC” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are manual accruals either reversing or accruing for costs incurred. The Summary of Selected Operating 
Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 11 AC accruing entries and largest 4 AC reversing 
entries (measured by absolute dollar impact on expense), in addition to 1 AC entry selected without bias 
from intermediate months, and excluding amounts accruing and reversing in the twelve-month period.  
 
 
Amounts presented as “Y3” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are automated entries to record permit expenses.  
 
 
Amounts presented as “ZN” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are manual entries to record the amortization of fees imposed under New York State’s Public Service Law 
Section 18-a. The Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries includes the largest 14 
ZN entries recorded during the period. 
 
 
Amounts presented as “02” entries in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger Entries 
are credit entries to the income statement of the Company to reflect the allocation to the relevant 
counterparty of affiliate costs paid for by the Company. The other side of such entries is to an 
intercompany receivable. 
 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 57 of 69
227


--- Page 234 ---
System-generated settlement entries recorded under doc type ST or SA are not considered for inclusion in the 
Summary of Costs as they are not typically subject to manual intervention. Entries recorded under doc type Z4 
(allowance for funds used during construction), doc type ZC (depreciation), doc type RB (bad debt expense), 
doc type RV (miscellaneous customer billings), and doc type ZK (regulatory amortizations) are not considered 
for inclusion in the Summary of Costs as they are related to costs separately calculated without reference to 
historical data or recovered via mechanisms outside of base distribution rates. Entries recorded under doc 
types WA and WL represent materials inventory recorded as costs when used; such costs are recorded at the 
same amount as the item was purchased (the purchase is initially recorded under a KR or RE entry - see 
description above and Note 3). Entries recorded under the doc types 01, DG, IG, PR, PS, SK, WI, Y1, Y5, YO, Z5, 
Z6, ZJ, ZQ, and ZU aggregate under each individual doc type to an amount less than $3.5 million for the twelve-
month period presented and have not been included in the Summary of Costs.  
 
Basis of accounting – Amounts presented in the Summary of Selected Operating Expense General Ledger 
Entries are amounts recorded in the Companies’ general ledger using the doc types listed during the period 
presented. The amounts presented represent the following: 
 
 
AB – Amounts presented as AB entries have been accurately recorded to reflect the underlying 
transaction. 
 
AC –Amounts presented as AC entries have been accurately calculated and recorded to reflect the accrual 
in the appropriate period of the underlying transaction. 
 
Y3 –Amounts presented as Y3 entries have been accurately calculated to record the related permit 
expense. 
 
ZN –Amounts presented as ZN entries have been accurately calculated to record the amortization of the 
Company’s 18-a fee assessments. 
 
02 – Amounts presented as 02 entries have been accurately recorded to reflect transfer of the cost to an 
affiliate. 
 
7. Costs incurred and allocated by Servco 
Cost Allocation Manual and Service Agreements  
Costs incurred by Servco and by other NGUSA subsidiaries in providing services to NGUSA client companies 
(Client Companies), including the Company, are set forth in the service agreements and its Cost Allocation 
Manual (Revised December 2014) (CAM) and include, but are not limited to, labor and related overhead costs 
(e.g., salaries of officers and other employees, employee welfare expenses such as social security taxes, life 
insurance, pensions, post-retirement benefits other than pension, medical, dental and other welfare 
expenses), training costs, facilities costs, information technology costs, and costs related to vendor and other 
contract services. The organization, conduct of business and method of cost allocation are designed to result 
in the performance of services and the provision of goods economically and efficiently for the benefit of the 
Client Companies at cost, fairly and equitably allocated among such companies. 
The purpose of the CAM is to serve as a guide as to the manner in which costs should be charged to the Client 
Companies by the company incurring the cost.  The prevailing premise of these cost allocation guidelines is 
that allocation methods should not result in subsidization of non-regulated services or products by regulated 
entities or subsidization of services or products from one regulated entity to another.   
The provision of administrative services to the Client Companies by Servco is specified in service agreements 
filed with the respective utility regulatory commissions. 
Principles of cost allocation 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 58 of 69
228


--- Page 235 ---
The following principles guide the allocation of costs of products or services provided by Servco and other 
operating subsidiaries to the Client Companies.  
 
Direct charging or direct assignment is the preferred allocation methodology and is used if the cost of 
providing a product or service can be identified with the Client Companies receiving the benefit of that 
product or service. Direct charging should only be used if the cost of providing a product or service to an 
individual Client Company can be isolated and reported separately from costs to provide other products 
or services and from costs to provide the same product and service to other Client Companies. 
 
The costs of products and services that cannot be direct charged should be allocated based on cost 
causative allocation bases representative of the underlying cost drivers of that product or service. 
 
The cost allocation methodology should be comprehensive, transparent, stable and administratively 
manageable and cost effective.   
 
The calculation of the cost allocation bases should be supported by a clearly defined methodology, model 
and supporting policy and procedure documentation.  
 
The cost allocation methodology should accommodate changes in the size of the allocation bases from 
period to period based on changes in the underlying cost drivers; the allocation bases should not vary 
significantly from period to period for uncontrollable factors not related to the underlying cost drivers.   
 
The calculation of the allocation bases should be updated at least annually and more frequently if needed 
to reflect significant events (e.g., the sale of a significant affiliate). 
Costs are defined among the following four categories for purposes of allocating the costs of products and 
services to the Client Companies.  This hierarchy represents the order of methodologies used when allocating 
costs.  These four cost categories are: 
 
Directly Assignable – Expenses incurred for activities and services exclusively for the benefit of a Client 
Company.   
 
Directly Attributable – Expenses incurred for activities and services that benefit more than one Client 
Company and which can be allocated based on direct measures of cost causation; for example number of 
employees or number of customers. 
 
Indirectly Attributable – Expenses incurred as a “cost of doing business” that do not relate to the 
provision of specific products and services.  The costs typically benefit all entities within the corporate 
umbrella.  Examples include governance costs, Corporate Secretary costs, and investor relations costs. 
These costs are typically allocated based on a general measure of cost causation, commonly referred to as 
the general allocator. Costs that cannot be directly identified with the provision of a particular product or 
service. This includes but is not limited to governance costs, insurance, and taxes as well the cost of 
services supporting Servco such as Servco accounting and recruiting for Servco positions.  The general 
allocator is the ratio of net plant, net margins and net operations and maintenance expenses, equally 
weighted. 
 
Unattributable – Expenses incurred for activities or services that have been determined as not 
appropriate for apportionment to the Client Companies.  These costs relate primarily to activities such as 
corporate diversification, political or philanthropic endeavors and, as such, are charged directly to the 
parent company, NGUSA. 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 2 
                 Page 59 of 69
229


--- Page 236 ---
To apply these principles, Servco and the Client Companies accumulate costs in system orders. The order 
design acts as the primary cost control element from which costs are billed to affiliates. For each order, an 
order design documents a description of the order’s use and purpose, the activities associated with that 
particular order, and justification for the allocation methodology to be used for allocating the costs 
accumulated in the order. Only one allocation methodology can be used for each order.  
Subsidiaries charge no more than actual costs for services provided to the Company and other regulated 
affiliates. The monthly settlements process includes only the costs accumulated in the open orders. There is 
no markup or profit included in allocations to the regulated affiliates. The allocations are based on the 
methodologies designated and described above. Accordingly, the unit cost (price) charged to these affiliates, 
including the Company, is intended to reasonably approximate the actual costs of providing such services.  
Section 7 of the CAM lists those services provided by Servco and the Client Companies to whom these services 
are provided. These services are provided in accordance with the service agreements filed with the respective 
utility regulatory commissions.

COST IMPACTS OF THE  
LONG ISLAND POWER AUTHORITY SEPARATION

Introduction 
 
On October 2, 2014, the New York State Public Service Commission issued the audit report 
prepared by NorthStar Consulting Group (“NorthStar”) regarding its Comprehensive 
Management and Operations Audit of National Grid USA’s (“National Grid” or “Company”) 
three New York gas companies.  In response, The Brooklyn Union Gas Company d/b/a 
National Grid NY (“KEDNY”), KeySpan Gas East Corporation d/b/a National Grid (“KEDLI”) 
and Niagara Mohawk Power Corporation d/b/a National Grid (“NMPC”) (collectively, the “NY 
Gas Companies”) submitted an implementation plan dated November 3, 2014 (“Implementation 
Plan”).  The NY Gas Companies submit this report pursuant to the Implementation Plan and 
specifically in response to NorthStar’s Recommendation III-6, which states:  
 
Conduct, or contract with qualified outside auditor for, an investigation into the cost 
impacts of the LIPA separation on remaining NY operating company ratepayers, and 
provide a report to the PSC staff within six months.  The investigation should include: 
• True stranded costs broken out by labor and non-labor sources, and by capital 
investments and operating charges 
• Expected revenues by basis (e.g., real estate, IT) and the time period for the revenue 
agreement. 
• Incremental costs, both labor and non-labor, and capital and on-going operating costs 
or benefits 
• Allocated costs (labor and non-labor combined if necessary), and an estimate of the 
aggregate impact of the reallocation of these costs on the remaining NY utilities. 
The investigation should include a broad review of activities and functions that were 
performed with, or for the benefit of LIPA, rather than relying solely on prior listings of 
impacted areas. Where applicable and reasonable, existing studies can be used as sources 
for the cost estimates, with adjustments clearly specified.  The investigation seeks to define 
and document the costs associated with the separation for future use. 
 
 
Implementation Plan Leads  
Executive Sponsor: 
Sharon Partridge, Vice President – Financial Planning & 
Analysis & Decision Support 
David Doxsee, Vice President – Finance (New York) 
Team Lead(s): 
Chip Benson, Director – Finance Business Partners

I.  BACKGROUND   
In January 2014, the Long Island Power Authority (“LIPA”) transitioned to a new manager, Public 
Service Enterprise Group Long Island LLC (“PSEG-LI”), for its Long Island electric transmission 
and distribution (“T&D”) system.  For more than fifteen years prior to that transition, a National 
Grid affiliate, National Grid Electric Services LLC, had served as LIPA’s T&D manager under a 
long term contract (the Management Services Agreement (“MSA”)).  Under the MSA, National 
Grid provided a comprehensive set of electric operations (construction, maintenance, planning), 
customer (call handling, billing, collections) and support services (information systems, human 
resources, environmental, finance) for LIPA’s electric business.   
 
Long Island Gas Transition 
 
LIPA’s decision to select a new T&D manager necessitated the separation of National Grid’s 
customer, operations and other functions that had historically supported both the gas and electric 
businesses on Long Island.  Accordingly, the loss of the LIPA MSA resulted in the loss of certain 
operational efficiencies and cost synergies that had been realized by National Grid’s combined 
electric and gas functions.  Indeed, LIPA’s decision to select a new T&D manager forced 
National Grid to consider alternative solutions to cost-effectively meet the needs of its customers. 
 
National Grid developed a comprehensive customer service transition plan for the Long Island gas 
business that focused on enhancing service quality levels while also mitigating the cost impacts on 
KEDLI’s customers.  Several major customer initiatives were born out of the LIPA separation, 
including (i) a new customer information system that replaced the 40 year old legacy LILCO 
system and improved the billing functionality for KEDLI’s customers (with enhanced self-service 
functions and bill format/presentation); (ii) retrofitting 568,000 gas meters with automated meter 
reading devices that provide improved meter reading accuracy, support storm restoration and 
result in better overall customer satisfaction; (iii) relocating the Long Island customer call center 
operation to an existing National Grid customer call center in Brooklyn, New York (MetroTech); 
and (iv) an agreement with PSEG-LI to provide gas customers with continued access to the eleven 
Long Island customer offices.  While each of these programs presented various implementation 
challenges, they ultimately established a new customer platform for National Grid’s gas business 
on Long Island.  In the months leading to the transition, a customer outreach program was 
launched to educate KEDLI’s customers on the service changes, and re-introduce National Grid as 
the gas company on Long Island to promote gas growth. 
 
Support for LIPA’s Transition to PSEG-LI 
 
At the same time National Grid was planning for the future of its gas business post-separation, the 
Company was also focused on its commitments to support an orderly transition to PSEG-LI.  To 
that end, LIPA and PSEG-LI developed transition plans for every aspect of LIPA’s electric 
businesses, and National Grid dedicated the resources and transition support necessary to assist 
PSEG-LI’s execution of those plans. 
 
National Grid and LIPA worked to ensure their respective businesses were appropriately staffed 
with qualified personnel.  As a result, more than 2,000 former National Grid employees primarily 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 4 
                   Page 3 of 14
246


--- Page 253 ---
4 
 
supporting electric operations on Long Island transferred to PSEG-LI so the Long Island electric 
businesses would continue to benefit from an experienced workforce. 
 
With regard to the IT systems supporting electric operations on Long Island, PSEG-LI initially 
utilized various National Grid systems under a transition arrangement.  Over a period of several 
months, National Grid supported PSEG-LI’s efforts to transition more than 700 business systems 
(and associated data), including systems supporting back office, work management, the call 
center, CRM (Customer Relationship Management) function, Data Warehouse (Collections, 
Metering and Marketing), document management, metering, electric T&D operations, and 
SCADA (Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition).  Each system transition was closely 
coordinated with PSEG-LI and was executed without significant disruption to the LIPA end users.  
In addition, National Grid transitioned approximately 1,500 email accounts, multiple 
infrastructure hardware assets, network connections, and various LIPA information technology 
vendor contracts. 
 
To support future storm restoration efforts on Long Island, National Grid and PSEG-LI agreed that 
certain gas field personnel not engaged in gas restoration would be made available to assist with 
PSEG-LI’s storm response. 
 
NY Gas Management Audit – LIPA Transition Costs 
 
The Audit Report identified the following potential costs arising from the separation of LIPA and 
National Grid’s operations: 
• Stranded costs associated with investments or activities specific to LIPA that cannot 
be eliminated or could not be transferred to PSEG-LI.   
• Incremental costs to replace systems or personnel that previously had enjoyed 
efficiencies of scale or that were transferred to LIPA and must be replaced.   
• Administrative and overhead costs that are spread across multiple operating 
companies through the general cost allocation processes.1 
During audit discovery (approximately six months prior to the separation), National Grid 
estimated the total cost impacts of the LIPA separation at approximately $18.5 million.2  Now 
that the transition has been completed (as of January 1, 2014), the auditors recommended that the 
Company refresh its analysis to identify and quantify the total cost impacts on the NY Gas 
Companies with reasonable accuracy. 
This report summarizes the various incremental transition costs incurred by National Grid in 
connection with the LIPA separation, and as well as the impact on National Grid’s cost 
allocations resulting from the loss of the MSA.     
 
 
                                                          
1 Audit Report, Chapter III, p.30. 
2 See Data Request 140, Attachment 7.

II.  COST IMPACTS OF THE LIPA SEPARATION 
 
As described in the Implementation Plan, the Company has updated its analysis of the stranded, 
incremental and re-allocated costs resulting from the loss of the of the LIPA management 
agreement.  The Company’s analysis includes an assessment of the following transition cost 
items: 
1. 
An accounting of stranded costs (capital and O&M) resulting from the LIPA transition 
broken out by labor, systems, facilities, etc.; 
2. 
Any incremental costs (labor and non-labor) and capital and on-going operating costs 
or benefits incurred as a result of the loss of the LIPA agreement; 
3. 
An estimate of the aggregate impact of  reallocated costs on the New York Operating 
Companies; and 
4. 
Offsetting LIPA transition charges paid by PSEG-LI. 
The Company’s revised analysis has identified approximately $17 million in one-time, 
non-recurring separation costs incurred in FY15, including the cost to replace facilities and 
systems that were transferred to LIPA (e.g., the customer call center).  In addition, the analysis 
identified approximately $20.8 million in allocated costs to National Grid’s operating companies 
resulting from the separation, of which approximately $10.9 million is allocable to the New York 
utilities (gas and electric).  The increase in costs to National Grid’s other operating companies 
is largely attributable to a reallocation of costs for shared services, human resources, information 
systems and procurement support, which had been charged to LIPA through the MSA.    
 
 
 
1.  Stranded Costs 
 
For purposes of this analysis, National Grid defined “stranded costs” as costs incurred to support 
LIPA only (e.g., the cost of support services with no benefit to any National Grid operating 
company) that could not be eliminated or transferred to PSEG-LI.3 
 
In the months following termination of the MSA, National Grid successfully transitioned and/or 
eliminated all costs incurred solely to support LIPA.  Accordingly, the Company’s analysis did 
not identify any truly stranded costs.   
 
 
 
 
 
                                                          
3 In Data Request 140 (Attachment 7), the Company used the term “stranded costs” more broadly to 
include any labor and non-labor cost impacts on National Grid’s other operating companies. 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 4 
                   Page 5 of 14
248


--- Page 255 ---
6 
 
2.  Incremental Costs 
 
 
“Incremental costs” are costs incurred by the Company to facilitate the separation of the electric 
T&D management business and support National Grid’s operating companies following the 
transition.  These incremental costs include any cost incurred in the functional areas to replace 
facilities and/or systems that transferred to LIPA or PSEG-LI, as well as costs increases resulting 
from a loss of operational efficiencies (particularly with respect to the gas business on Long 
Island).  Attachment 1 sets forth the incremental costs incurred by National Grid as a result of the 
LIPA separation. 
 
 
A.  Customer Service.   
 
The management services under the MSA included customer service support that was 
provided to LIPA through National Grid’s customer service organization.  LIPA’s 
decision to select a new manager for its T&D system necessitated the separation of 
National Grid’s customer function supporting the gas and electric businesses on Long 
Island.   
 
As part of the transition, most of the Long Island customer facilities (Melville Call Center, 
customer offices) were leased to LIPA/PSEG-LI; and the customer service personnel (call 
center representatives, customer office staff, meter readers) transferred to PSEG-LI.  
Accordingly, to provide customer service support to National Grid’s Long Island gas 
business (KeySpan Gas East or “KEDLI”) following the transition, the Company looked to 
leverage additional synergies through its other downstate New York gas business 
(“KEDNY”) and to implement other cost effective solutions.   
 
To support KEDLI’s gas business following the transition, National Grid was required to 
invest in new systems, facilities and equipment to replace the customer service functions 
on Long Island, including: 
   
• Meter Reading.   Prior to the LIPA separation, gas and electric meters were jointly 
read by meter reading personnel employed by National Grid.  These meter readers 
transitioned to PSEG-LI in 2014.  KEDLI evaluated the cost and benefits of the 
various meter reading options and determined that an automated meter reading 
(“AMR”) program was the most cost effective, long term solution to reading gas 
meters on Long Island.  In addition to cost benefits, KEDLI’s gas customer will 
also benefit from services enhancements (e.g., more timely and accurate meter 
reads; enhanced storm response) from AMR deployment.  
 
While the timing of the AMR deployment was driven by the Company’s need to 
develop an alternate meter reading solution following the transition of the physical 
meter reading workforce to PSEG-LI, the AMR investment is a long-term customer 
service enhancement that the Company does not consider a LIPA transition cost.  
To facilitate the transition to AMR, KEDLI engaged a contractor to perform gas 
only, physical meter reads on Long Island over a period of several months while the 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 4 
                   Page 6 of 14
249


--- Page 256 ---
7 
 
Company deployed the AMR system.  The incremental costs to manually read gas 
meters during the AMR deployment are set forth in Attachment 1.   
 
• Customer Call Center.  Because the call center used to support KEDLI and LIPA 
transitioned to PSEG-LI, KEDLI initially relocated its customer call center 
operation to a temporary facility on Long Island (National Grid’s Melville, Hub 
Drive facility).  In 2014, the call center function was permanently transitioned to 
an existing National Grid customer call center in Brooklyn, New York 
(MetroTech).  To accommodate the transition, National Grid incurred costs to (i) 
establish a temporary Hub Drive call center, (ii) hire and train new call center 
representatives – initially to work at the temporary Hub Drive and later for 
MetroTech, and (iii) construct a permanent KEDLI call center in MetroTech.  
 
The transition, facilities and other costs to establish a new KEDLI call center at 
MetroTech are set forth in the table below and Attachment 1.   
 
• Customer Offices.  KEDLI and LIPA jointly operated eleven walk-in customer 
offices on Long Island for electric and gas customers.  These offices were staffed 
with

twenty-four months.  As part of the transition services arrangement, PSEG agreed 
to accept payments from KEDLI customers at each of the customer offices and 
provide space for National Grid interview tellers at the busiest gas customer 
offices.
