B.   Gas Operations   
 
As part of the LIPA separation analysis, the Company identified the following cost impacts 
on operations resulting from the separation: 
   
• Warehouse and Vehicles - Inventory/warehouse services for gas and electric 
operations on Long Island had been provided out of a central distribution facility 
in Hicksville, New York.  Following the transition, the Company transitioned 
these functions to National Grid’s existing Greenpoint warehouse facility, which 
required upgrades to accommodate the additional inventory. National Grid also 
procured additional vehicles to support its new inventory distribution process.  
 
 
 
 
 
 
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 4 
                   Page 8 of 14
251


--- Page 258 ---
9 
 
Table 2: Incremental Operations Costs – Warehouse & Vehicles   
 
         
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
• Gas Control Room - The Downstate Gas Control Center is currently operated out 
of a joint gas/electric facility in Hicksville.  While National Grid operated a joint 
electric and gas control room, space constraints in the Hicksville location, as well 
as physical and cybersecurity considerations, warranted the separation of the 
electric and gas control rooms following the LIPA transition.  The timing of the 
relocation was influenced by the LIPA transition, but the decision to relocate and 
upgrade the Gas Control Center was principally driven by operational 
considerations, specifically the need to enhance control room capabilities to 
support New York gas operations.   
 
In October 2015, Gas Control will move to a dedicated gas facility in Melville 
(Hub Drive), which is currently under construction.  The new center will monitor 
and control the gas system for both the upstate (NMPC) and the downstate 
(KEDNY, KEDLI) regions.  The Control Center will also be connected to 
National Grid’s Northborough (Mass) control center to provide enhanced business 
continuity for emergency operations.  Because the investment in a new gas 
control room was based on operational considerations, the Company does not 
consider the associated costs as Incremental Costs for purposes of this analysis.     
 
C. Property Costs – Segmentation 
National Grid owns various operating facilities across Long Island that were historically 
used to support electric and gas operations (e.g., operating yards in Hicksville, 
Brentwood, Hewlett, Roslyn and Riverhead).  The Company incurred facilities costs at 
eight sites to accommodate the physical separation of the gas and electric businesses.  
These costs include site renovations necessary to segregate operations, security, badging, 
communications, etc.  Pursuant to an agreement between the parties, National Grid and 
LIPA shared (50/50) in these facilities costs.  A breakdown of the segmentation costs 
are set forth on Attachment 1.

D. Miscellaneous 
National Grid incurred a number of miscellaneous costs to facilitate the transition in 
various functional areas.  These include the costs of temporary facilities to 
accommodate the transition team, communications costs incurred for functions 
transitioning to new facilities, and the cost of electricity at National Grid facilities.  The 
material miscellaneous costs are summarized in Attachment 1.    
 
3.  Dis-Synergy/Allocated Costs 
Notwithstanding the termination of the MSA, National Grid continues to incur certain costs that 
had been partially allocated to LIPA but cannot be eliminated, which NorthStar characterized as 
“[a]dministrative and overhead costs that are spread across multiple operating companies 
through the general cost allocation processes.”  These include service company charges for 
items like human resources, treasury and information services that cannot be proportionally 
reduced or eliminated in response to the loss of the MSA.   
The Company’s analysis looked to quantify the allocated costs from internal support functions 
that could not be reduced following the LIPA separation and, as a result, were allocated to the 
other operating companies.   
In FY15, the total costs reallocated from LIPA to other operating companies were $20.8 million, 
of which $10.9 million were allocated to the New York utilities.  Attachment 2 sets forth 
National Grid’s allocated costs by category, and further broken down by labor, materials, 
consultants, contractors, etc.   
 
4.  Transition Services Agreement and Other Revenues 
Pursuant to a Transition Services Agreement (“TSA”) between National Grid and LIPA, 
National Grid provided LIPA and PSEG-LI with certain information technology services, 
business process services, and access to National Grid’s software on a transitional basis.  The 
fees billed by National Grid for the transition services included recovery of the operating 
expenses incurred to provide the services, as well as a return on National Grid’s capital 
investments in the systems supporting the TSA.  National Grid invoices LIPA/PSEG-LI based 
on estimated costs to provide the various TSA services, and the invoices are subsequently 
reconciled to actual costs.  TSA revenues by category are presented in Attachment 3. 
The TSA terminated with respect to most transition services in FY15; however, National Grid 
continues to provide certain IS and other support, including: business application services, data 
center services, network support and emergency response services.  The Company anticipates 
providing LIPA this limited set of transition services through November 2015. 
 
   
Exhibit________(RRP-2) 
                      Schedule 4 
                  Page 10 of 14
253


--- Page 260 ---

IV.  CONCLUSION 
Pursuant to Recommendation III-6 in the Audit Report, National Grid refreshed its analysis of 
the cost impacts on the NY Gas Companies resulting from the LIPA separation.  National Grid 
was able to transition/eliminate costs incurred solely for purposes of serving LIPA under the 
MSA and, therefore, the transition did not result in any stranded costs.  However, National Grid 
did incur incremental facilities and systems costs in connection with the transition, totaling 
approximately $17 million in FY15.  In addition, the loss of the MSA created certain 
dis-synergies and, as a result, the New York utilities were allocated an additional $10.9 million 
in FY15.
